It defines the interface between user space and kernel space.\n\n## File Structure\n\n### .txt files (syscall descriptions)\nContains the actual syscall and ioctl descriptions in syzlang syntax.\n\n### .txt.const files (constant values)\nContains architecture-specific constant values for flags, ioctl numbers, etc.\n\n## Basic Syntax\n\n### Include Headers\n```\ninclude <uapi/linux/header.h>\ninclude <uapi/asm/ioctl.h>\n```\n\n### Resources (File Descriptors)\nResources represent typed file descriptors:\n```\nresource fd_type[fd]\n```\n\n### Device Openers\nUse `syz_open_dev` for character devices:\n```\nsyz_open_dev$name(dev ptr[in, string[\"/dev/device#\"]], id intptr, flags flags[open_flags]) fd_type\n```\n- The `#` in the device path is replaced with numbers during fuzzing\n- Returns the resource type (fd_type)\n\n### Ioctl Definitions\n```\nioctl$NAME(fd fd_type, cmd const[IOCTL_NAME], arg ptr[in/out/inout, struct_type])\n```\n\nDirection indicators:\n- `ptr[in, type]` - input only (user → kernel)\n- `ptr[out, type]` - output only (kernel → user)\n- `ptr[inout, type]` - bidirectional\n- For ioctls with no argument, use `arg const[0, intptr]`\n\n### Structures\n```\nstruct_name {\n    field1    type\n    field2    flags[flag_set, int32]\n    field3    array[int8, 64]\n}\n```\n\nCommon types:\n- `int8`, `int16`, `int32`, `int64` - signed integers\n- `intptr` - pointer-sized integer\n- `array[type, size]` - fixed-size array\n- `flags[name, base_type]` - flags from a set\n- `const[value, type]` - constant value\n\n### Flag Sets\n```\nflag_name = FLAG1, FLAG2, FLAG3\n```\n\n### Defines (for custom ioctl numbers)\n```\ndefine IOCTL_NAME    _IOR/_IOW/_IOWR(magic, nr, type)\n```\n\n## Ioctl Directions (_IOR, _IOW, _IOWR, _IO)\n\nFrom Linux kernel perspective:\n- `_IO(magic, nr)` - no argument\n- `_IOR(magic, nr, type)` - read (kernel → user, use `ptr[out]`)\n- `_IOW(magic, nr, type)` - write (user → kernel, use `ptr[in]`)\n- `_IOWR(magic, nr, type)` - read/write (bidirectional, use `ptr[inout]`)\n\n## Example: Complete Device Description\n\n```\n# Includes\ninclude <uapi/linux/mydev.h>\ninclude <uapi/asm/ioctl.h>\n\n# Resource\nresource fd_mydev[fd]\n\n# Opener\nsyz_open_dev$mydev(dev ptr[in, string[\"/dev/mydev#\"]], id intptr, flags flags[open_flags]) fd_mydev\n\n# Ioctls\nioctl$MYDEV_GET_INFO(fd fd_mydev, cmd const[MYDEV_GET_INFO], arg ptr[out, mydev_info])\nioctl$MYDEV_SET_MODE(fd fd_mydev, cmd const[MYDEV_SET_MODE], arg ptr[in, int32])\nioctl$MYDEV_RESET(fd fd_mydev, cmd const[MYDEV_RESET], arg const[0, intptr])\n\n# Structures\nmydev_info {\n    version    int32\n    flags      flags[mydev_flags, int32]\n    reserved   array[const[0, int8], 32]\n}\n\n# Flags\nmydev_flags = MYDEV_FLAG_ENABLE, MYDEV_FLAG_DEBUG\n```\n\n## Const File Format\n\nThe `.txt.const` file is generated by syzkaller but can be manually created:\n\n```\n# Code generated by syz-sysgen. DO NOT EDIT.\narches = 386, amd64, arm, arm64\nIOCTL_NAME = value\nFLAG_NAME = value\n__NR_syscall = syscall_number, arch:specific_number\n```\n\n- Multiple architectures separated by colons for different values\n- Default value comes first, architecture-specific overrides follow\n\n## Best Practices\n\n1. Always read existing similar descriptions as examples\n2. Match ioctl directions (_IOR/_IOW/_IOWR) to ptr[in/out/inout]\n3. Use `const[0, ...]` for fields that should be zero-initialized\n4. Include all relevant flags and modes from kernel headers\n5. Test with `make descriptions` and `make all` after creating descriptions\n"}}],"stop_reason":null,"stop_sequence":null,"usage":{"input_tokens":5,"cache_creation_input_tokens":110,"cache_read_input_tokens":26613,"cache_creation":{"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":110,"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":0},"output_tokens":25},"context_management":null},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"a3561076-f667-4b59-856a-71e5fff29ad2","uuid":"54b9d61f-f552-4299-be52-fbb109a88263"}

{"type":"assistant","message":{"model":"claude-sonnet-4-5-20250929","id":"msg_vrtx_01EwnPLyfNPeP5CXt8UZPxyZ","type":"message","role":"assistant","content":[{"type":"tool_use","id":"toolu_vrtx_01B2x2aLNsu678PorYXt7soE","name":"Write","input":{"file_path":"/root/environment/skills/linux-ioctl-analysis.md","content":"# Linux Ioctl Analysis\n\n## Overview\nUnderstanding Linux ioctl commands is crucial for creating syzkaller descriptions. Ioctls provide device-specific control operations.\n\n## Ioctl Structure\n\nAn ioctl is defined by:\n1. **Magic number** - Character identifying the device subsystem\n2. **Sequence number** - Unique number for each ioctl\n3. **Direction** - Data transfer direction\n4. **Argument type** - Structure or primitive type\n\n## Ioctl Macros\n\n### Basic Macros (linux/ioctl.h)\n\n```c\n_IO(magic, nr)           // No argument\n_IOR(magic, nr, type)    // Read from device (kernel → user)\n_IOW(magic, nr, type)    // Write to device (user → kernel)\n_IOWR(magic, nr, type)   // Read and Write (bidirectional)\n```\n\n### Direction Understanding\n\n**Important:** The direction is from the kernel's perspective:\n- `_IOR` - Kernel reads from device and writes to user buffer (user receives data)\n- `_IOW` - Kernel reads from user buffer and writes to device (user sends data)\n- `_IOWR` - Both read and write\n\n### Examples from ppdev.h\n\n```c\n#define PPSETMODE   _IOW(PP_IOCTL, 0x80, int)\n// User → Kernel: User sets a mode value\n\n#define PPRSTATUS   _IOR(PP_IOCTL, 0x81, unsigned char)\n// Kernel → User: User reads status byte\n\n#define PPGETTIME   _IOR(PP_IOCTL, 0x95, struct timeval)\n// Kernel → User: User gets timeout value\n\n#define PPSETTIME   _IOW(PP_IOCTL, 0x96, struct timeval)\n// User → Kernel: User sets timeout value\n\n#define PPCLAIM     _IO(PP_IOCTL, 0x8b)\n// No argument: Just claims the port\n```\n\n## Mapping to Syzkaller\n\n### Ioctl Direction → ptr[] Direction\n\n| Kernel Macro | Syzkaller Direction | Meaning |\n|--------------|---------------------|---------|\n| `_IO` | `const[0, intptr]` | No argument |\n| `_IOR` | `ptr[out, type]` | Kernel writes to user |\n| `_IOW` | `ptr[in, type]` | User writes to kernel |\n| `_IOWR` | `ptr[inout, type]` | Bidirectional |\n\n### Complete Example\n\nKernel header:\n```c\n#define PP_IOCTL 'p'\n#define PPRSTATUS _IOR(PP_IOCTL, 0x81, unsigned char)\n```\n\nSyzkaller description:\n```\nioctl$PPRSTATUS(fd fd_ppdev, cmd const[PPRSTATUS], arg ptr[out, int8])\n```\n\n## Analyzing Ioctl Arguments\n\n### Primitive Types\n```c\n_IOW(MAGIC, NR, int)              → ptr[in, int32]\n_IOR(MAGIC, NR, unsigned char)    → ptr[out, int8]\n_IOW(MAGIC, NR, unsigned int)     → ptr[in, int32]\n```\n\n### Structure Types\n```c\nstruct mydev_data {\n    unsigned char mask;\n    unsigned char val;\n};\n_IOW(MAGIC, NR, struct mydev_data) → ptr[in, mydev_data]\n```\n\nSyzkaller structure:\n```\nmydev_data {\n    mask    int8\n    val     int8\n}\n```\n\n### Special Cases\n\n**OBSOLETE ioctls:**\nSkip these in syzkaller descriptions - they're deprecated.\n\n**struct timeval:**\nAlready defined in syzkaller, use `timeval` directly:\n```\nioctl$PPGETTIME(fd fd_ppdev, cmd const[PPGETTIME], arg ptr[out, timeval])\n```\n\n## Extracting Ioctl Information\n\n### From Header Files\n```bash\n# Find all ioctls\ngrep -E \"^#define\\s+\\w+\\s+_IO\" /usr/include/linux/ppdev.h\n\n# Count non-obsolete ioctls\ngrep -E \"^#define\\s+\\w+\\s+_IO\" /usr/include/linux/ppdev.h | grep -v OBSOLETE | wc -l\n```\n\n### Understanding the Magic Number\n```c\n#define PP_IOCTL 'p'\n```\nThe magic 'p' identifies this as the parallel port subsystem.\n\n## Common Patterns\n\n### No-argument ioctls (control operations)\n```c\n#define PPCLAIM    _IO(PP_IOCTL, 0x8b)\n#define PPRELEASE  _IO(PP_IOCTL, 0x8c)\n```\n→ `arg const[0, intptr]`\n\n### Status/data read ioctls\n```c\n#define PPRSTATUS  _IOR(PP_IOCTL, 0x81, unsigned char)\n#define PPRDATA    _IOR(PP_IOCTL, 0x85, unsigned char)\n```\n→ `arg ptr[out, int8]`\n\n### Configuration ioctls\n```c\n#define PPSETMODE  _IOW(PP_IOCTL, 0x80, int)\n#define PPDATADIR  _IOW(PP_IOCTL, 0x90, int)\n```\n→ `arg ptr[in, int32]`\n\n### Frob (modify bits) ioctls\n```c\nstruct ppdev_frob_struct {\n    unsigned char mask;\n    unsigned char val;\n};\n#define PPFCONTROL _IOW(PP_IOCTL, 0x8e, struct ppdev_frob_struct)\n```\n→ `arg ptr[in, ppdev_frob_struct]`\n\n## Verification\n\nAfter creating descriptions:\n1. Check that all non-OBSOLETE ioctls are included\n2. Verify direction matches (_IOR → ptr[out], _IOW → ptr[in])\n3. Ensure structures match kernel definitions exactly\n4. Test with syzkaller build system\n"}}],"stop_reason":null,"stop_sequence":null,"usage":{"input_tokens":4,"cache_creation_input_tokens":1359,"cache_read_input_tokens":26723,"cache_creation":{"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":1359,"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":0},"output_tokens":18},"context_management":null},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"a3561076-f667-4b59-856a-71e5fff29ad2","uuid":"df9a1ccf-f2e5-4253-b325-af95492b389a"}

Ioctls provide device-specific control operations.\n\n## Ioctl Structure\n\nAn ioctl is defined by:\n1. **Magic number** - Character identifying the device subsystem\n2. **Sequence number** - Unique number for each ioctl\n3. **Direction** - Data transfer direction\n4. **Argument type** - Structure or primitive type\n\n## Ioctl Macros\n\n### Basic Macros (linux/ioctl.h)\n\n```c\n_IO(magic, nr)           // No argument\n_IOR(magic, nr, type)    // Read from device (kernel → user)\n_IOW(magic, nr, type)    // Write to device (user → kernel)\n_IOWR(magic, nr, type)   // Read and Write (bidirectional)\n```\n\n### Direction Understanding\n\n**Important:** The direction is from the kernel's perspective:\n- `_IOR` - Kernel reads from device and writes to user buffer (user receives data)\n- `_IOW` - Kernel reads from user buffer and writes to device (user sends data)\n- `_IOWR` - Both read and write\n\n### Examples from ppdev.h\n\n```c\n#define PPSETMODE   _IOW(PP_IOCTL, 0x80, int)\n// User → Kernel: User sets a mode value\n\n#define PPRSTATUS   _IOR(PP_IOCTL, 0x81, unsigned char)\n// Kernel → User: User reads status byte\n\n#define PPGETTIME   _IOR(PP_IOCTL, 0x95, struct timeval)\n// Kernel → User: User gets timeout value\n\n#define PPSETTIME   _IOW(PP_IOCTL, 0x96, struct timeval)\n// User → Kernel: User sets timeout value\n\n#define PPCLAIM     _IO(PP_IOCTL, 0x8b)\n// No argument: Just claims the port\n```\n\n## Mapping to Syzkaller\n\n### Ioctl Direction → ptr[] Direction\n\n| Kernel Macro | Syzkaller Direction | Meaning |\n|--------------|---------------------|---------|\n| `_IO` | `const[0, intptr]` | No argument |\n| `_IOR` | `ptr[out, type]` | Kernel writes to user |\n| `_IOW` | `ptr[in, type]` | User writes to kernel |\n| `_IOWR` | `ptr[inout, type]` | Bidirectional |\n\n### Complete Example\n\nKernel header:\n```c\n#define PP_IOCTL 'p'\n#define PPRSTATUS _IOR(PP_IOCTL, 0x81, unsigned char)\n```\n\nSyzkaller description:\n```\nioctl$PPRSTATUS(fd fd_ppdev, cmd const[PPRSTATUS], arg ptr[out, int8])\n```\n\n## Analyzing Ioctl Arguments\n\n### Primitive Types\n```c\n_IOW(MAGIC, NR, int)              → ptr[in, int32]\n_IOR(MAGIC, NR, unsigned char)    → ptr[out, int8]\n_IOW(MAGIC, NR, unsigned int)     → ptr[in, int32]\n```\n\n### Structure Types\n```c\nstruct mydev_data {\n    unsigned char mask;\n    unsigned char val;\n};\n_IOW(MAGIC, NR, struct mydev_data) → ptr[in, mydev_data]\n```\n\nSyzkaller structure:\n```\nmydev_data {\n    mask    int8\n    val     int8\n}\n```\n\n### Special Cases\n\n**OBSOLETE ioctls:**\nSkip these in syzkaller descriptions - they're deprecated.\n\n**struct timeval:**\nAlready defined in syzkaller, use `timeval` directly:\n```\nioctl$PPGETTIME(fd fd_ppdev, cmd const[PPGETTIME], arg ptr[out, timeval])\n```\n\n## Extracting Ioctl Information\n\n### From Header Files\n```bash\n# Find all ioctls\ngrep -E \"^#define\\s+\\w+\\s+_IO\" /usr/include/linux/ppdev.h\n\n# Count non-obsolete ioctls\ngrep -E \"^#define\\s+\\w+\\s+_IO\" /usr/include/linux/ppdev.h | grep -v OBSOLETE | wc -l\n```\n\n### Understanding the Magic Number\n```c\n#define PP_IOCTL 'p'\n```\nThe magic 'p' identifies this as the parallel port subsystem.\n\n## Common Patterns\n\n### No-argument ioctls (control operations)\n```c\n#define PPCLAIM    _IO(PP_IOCTL, 0x8b)\n#define PPRELEASE  _IO(PP_IOCTL, 0x8c)\n```\n→ `arg const[0, intptr]`\n\n### Status/data read ioctls\n```c\n#define PPRSTATUS  _IOR(PP_IOCTL, 0x81, unsigned char)\n#define PPRDATA    _IOR(PP_IOCTL, 0x85, unsigned char)\n```\n→ `arg ptr[out, int8]`\n\n### Configuration ioctls\n```c\n#define PPSETMODE  _IOW(PP_IOCTL, 0x80, int)\n#define PPDATADIR  _IOW(PP_IOCTL, 0x90, int)\n```\n→ `arg ptr[in, int32]`\n\n### Frob (modify bits) ioctls\n```c\nstruct ppdev_frob_struct {\n    unsigned char mask;\n    unsigned char val;\n};\n#define PPFCONTROL _IOW(PP_IOCTL, 0x8e, struct ppdev_frob_struct)\n```\n→ `arg ptr[in, ppdev_frob_struct]`\n\n## Verification\n\nAfter creating descriptions:\n1. Check that all non-OBSOLETE ioctls are included\n2. Verify direction matches (_IOR → ptr[out], _IOW → ptr[in])\n3. Ensure structures match kernel definitions exactly\n4. Test with syzkaller build system\n","structuredPatch":[],"originalFile":null}}

These files can be auto-generated or manually created.\n\n## File Format\n\n```\n# Code generated by syz-sysgen. DO NOT EDIT.\narches = 386, amd64, arm, arm64, mips64le, ppc64le, riscv64, s390x\nCONSTANT_NAME = value\nCONSTANT_NAME = default_value, arch1:arch2:value2, arch3:value3\n```\n\n## Architecture Specification\n\n### Common Architectures\n- `386` - 32-bit x86\n- `amd64` - 64-bit x86\n- `arm` - 32-bit ARM\n- `arm64` - 64-bit ARM\n- `mips64le` - MIPS 64-bit little-endian\n- `ppc64le` - PowerPC 64-bit little-endian\n- `riscv64` - RISC-V 64-bit\n- `s390x` - IBM System/390 64-bit\n\n### Architecture-Specific Values\n\nWhen constants differ across architectures:\n```\nIOCTL_NAME = default_value, arch1:value1, arch2:arch3:shared_value\n```\n\nExample:\n```\nSNDRV_RAWMIDI_IOCTL_PVERSION = 2147768064, mips64le:ppc64le:1074026240\n```\n- Default (386, amd64, arm, etc.): `2147768064`\n- mips64le and ppc64le: `1074026240`\n\n## Extracting Ioctl Values\n\n### Method 1: Using a C Program\n\nCreate a helper program:\n```c\n#include <stdio.h>\n#include <linux/ppdev.h>\n#include <linux/parport.h>\n\nint main() {\n    printf(\"PPSETMODE = %lu\\n\", (unsigned long)PPSETMODE);\n    printf(\"PPRSTATUS = %lu\\n\", (unsigned long)PPRSTATUS);\n    // ... all ioctls\n    printf(\"PP_FASTWRITE = %d\\n\", PP_FASTWRITE);\n    // ... all flags\n    return 0;\n}\n```\n\nCompile and run:\n```bash\ngcc -o extract_consts extract.c\n./extract_consts\n```\n\n### Method 2: Using Python ctypes\n\n```python\nimport ctypes\nimport os\n\n# Define ioctl encoding\n_IOC_NRBITS = 8\n_IOC_TYPEBITS = 8\n_IOC_SIZEBITS = 14\n_IOC_DIRBITS = 2\n\n_IOC_NRSHIFT = 0\n_IOC_TYPESHIFT = _IOC_NRSHIFT + _IOC_NRBITS\n_IOC_SIZESHIFT = _IOC_TYPESHIFT + _IOC_TYPEBITS\n_IOC_DIRSHIFT = _IOC_SIZESHIFT + _IOC_SIZEBITS\n\n_IOC_NONE = 0\n_IOC_WRITE = 1\n_IOC_READ = 2\n\ndef _IOC(dir, type, nr, size):\n    return (dir << _IOC_DIRSHIFT) | (ord(type) << _IOC_TYPESHIFT) | \\\n           (nr << _IOC_NRSHIFT) | (size << _IOC_SIZESHIFT)\n\ndef _IOR(type, nr, size):\n    return _IOC(_IOC_READ, type, nr, size)\n\ndef _IOW(type, nr, size):\n    return _IOC(_IOC_WRITE, type, nr, size)\n\n# Calculate ioctl values\nPP_IOCTL = 'p'\nprint(f\"PPSETMODE = {_IOW(PP_IOCTL, 0x80, 4)}\")\nprint(f\"PPRSTATUS = {_IOR(PP_IOCTL, 0x81, 1)}\")\n```\n\n### Method 3: From Existing Kernel\n\nUse the kernel source or installed headers:\n```bash\n# Search for the definition\ngrep -r \"PPSETMODE\" /usr/include/linux/\n\n# Use gcc to expand macros\necho '#include <linux/ppdev.h>' | gcc -E -dM - | grep PPSETMODE\n```\n\n## Common Constant Patterns\n\n### Ioctl Numbers\nTypically large numbers (> 1000000):\n```\nPPSETMODE = 1074032768\nPPRSTATUS = 2147776641\nPPCLAIM = 536896395\n```\n\n### Flag Values\nUsually small powers of 2 or combinations:\n```\nPP_FASTWRITE = 4\nPP_FASTREAD = 8\nPP_W91284PIC = 16\n```\n\n### IEEE1284 Modes\nCan be 0 or bit flags:\n```\nIEEE1284_MODE_NIBBLE = 0\nIEEE1284_MODE_BYTE = 1\nIEEE1284_MODE_COMPAT = 256\nIEEE1284_MODE_ECP = 16\nIEEE1284_MODE_EPP = 64\n```\n\n### Control/Status Bits\n```\nPARPORT_CONTROL_STROBE = 1\nPARPORT_CONTROL_AUTOFD = 2\nPARPORT_CONTROL_INIT = 4\nPARPORT_CONTROL_SELECT = 8\n```\n\n## Manual Const File Template\n\nFor a simple device with uniform architecture support:\n\n```\n# Code generated by syz-sysgen. DO NOT EDIT.\narches = 386, amd64\n\n# Ioctl numbers\nIOCTL_NAME1 = value1\nIOCTL_NAME2 = value2\n\n# Flags\nFLAG_NAME1 = value1\nFLAG_NAME2 = value2\n\n# Mode constants\nMODE_NAME1 = value1\nMODE_NAME2 = value2\n```\n\n## Verification\n\n### Verify Ioctl Values\n```bash\n# Compare with kernel headers\nprintf \"0x%x\\n\" $(echo '#include <linux/ppdev.h>\nPPSETMODE' | gcc -E -include stdio.h - | tail -1)\n```\n\n### Check for Architecture Differences\nDifferent architectures may have different:\n- Ioctl encodings (endianness affects _IOR/_IOW)\n- Structure padding/alignment\n- Syscall numbers\n\nUsually for x86-based systems (386, amd64), values are the same.\n\n## Example: Complete Const File\n\n```\n# Code generated by syz-sysgen. DO NOT EDIT.\narches = 386, amd64\n\n# Ioctl commands\nPPCLAIM = 536896395\nPPRELEASE = 536896396\nPPSETMODE = 1074032768\nPPRSTATUS = 2147776641\nPPWCONTROL = 1074032772\nPPRCONTROL = 2147776643\n\n# Flags\nPP_FASTWRITE = 4\nPP_FASTREAD = 8\nPP_W91284PIC = 16\n\n# IEEE1284 modes\nIEEE1284_MODE_NIBBLE = 0\nIEEE1284_MODE_BYTE = 1\nIEEE1284_MODE_COMPAT = 256\nIEEE1284_MODE_ECP = 16\nIEEE1284_MODE_EPP = 64\n```\n\n## Automation\n\nSyzkaller can auto-generate const files:\n```bash\ncd /opt/syzkaller\nmake extract TARGETOS=linux TARGETARCH=amd64\nmake generate\n```\n\nHowever, for new descriptions, manual creation is often necessary first.\n"}}],"stop_reason":null,"stop_sequence":null,"usage":{"input_tokens":4,"cache_creation_input_tokens":1650,"cache_read_input_tokens":28082,"cache_creation":{"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":1650,"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":0},"output_tokens":18},"context_management":null},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"a3561076-f667-4b59-856a-71e5fff29ad2","uuid":"b4c64263-ddfc-4c70-a72b-7f34951424b0"}

Syscall descriptions\n│       ├── *.txt.const     # Constant values\n│       └── *.txt.warn      # Warnings from generation\n├── bin/                    # Built binaries\n├── pkg/                    # Go packages\n└── Makefile               # Main build file\n```\n\n## Key Make Targets\n\n### 1. make descriptions\nParses and validates syscall descriptions without generating constants.\n\n```bash\ncd /opt/syzkaller\nmake descriptions\n```\n\n**What it does:**\n- Parses all .txt files in sys/linux/\n- Validates syntax\n- Checks for errors in structure definitions\n- Does NOT compile binaries\n\n**Use when:**\n- You've created/modified .txt files\n- You want to check syntax before generating constants\n- Quick validation\n\n### 2. make generate\nGenerates const files and updates descriptions.\n\n```bash\ncd /opt/syzkaller\nmake generate TARGETOS=linux TARGETARCH=amd64\n```\n\n**What it does:**\n- Extracts constants from kernel headers\n- Generates .txt.const files\n- Updates generated code\n\n**Use when:**\n- You have new .txt files needing .txt.const files\n- You've updated kernel headers\n\n### 3. make all\nFull build of syzkaller binaries.\n\n```bash\ncd /opt/syzkaller\nmake all TARGETOS=linux TARGETARCH=amd64\n```\n\n**What it does:**\n- Runs `make descriptions`\n- Compiles all Go code\n- Builds syz-manager, syz-fuzzer, syz-executor\n- Validates everything\n\n**Use when:**\n- Final verification\n- Before running fuzzer\n- After any changes\n\n### 4. make extract\nExtracts constants from kernel headers.\n\n```bash\ncd /opt/syzkaller\nmake extract TARGETOS=linux TARGETARCH=amd64\n```\n\n**Use when:**\n- Need to regenerate .txt.const files from scratch\n\n## Build Variables\n\n### TARGETOS\nOperating system target:\n- `linux` - Linux kernel\n- `freebsd` - FreeBSD\n- `netbsd` - NetBSD\n- etc.\n\n### TARGETARCH\nArchitecture target:\n- `amd64` - 64-bit x86\n- `386` - 32-bit x86\n- `arm64` - 64-bit ARM\n- `arm` - 32-bit ARM\n\n### Example\n```bash\nmake all TARGETOS=linux TARGETARCH=386\nmake all TARGETOS=linux TARGETARCH=amd64\n```\n\n## Common Workflows\n\n### Workflow 1: Adding New Device Description\n\n```bash\n# 1. Create .txt file\nvim /opt/syzkaller/sys/linux/dev_mydev.txt\n\n# 2. Validate syntax\ncd /opt/syzkaller\nmake descriptions\n\n# 3. Create .txt.const manually or generate\n# (Manual for this exercise)\n\n# 4. Full build to verify\nmake all TARGETOS=linux TARGETARCH=amd64\n```\n\n### Workflow 2: Updating Existing Description\n\n```bash\n# 1. Edit .txt file\nvim /opt/syzkaller/sys/linux/dev_mydev.txt\n\n# 2. Quick syntax check\nmake descriptions\n\n# 3. Full verification\nmake all TARGETOS=linux TARGETARCH=amd64\n```\n\n### Workflow 3: Multi-architecture Build\n\n```bash\n# Build for multiple architectures\nmake all TARGETOS=linux TARGETARCH=amd64\nmake all TARGETOS=linux TARGETARCH=386\nmake all TARGETOS=linux TARGETARCH=arm64\n```\n\n## Error Messages\n\n### Common Errors\n\n**\"unknown type\"**\n```\ndev_mydev.txt:10: unknown type 'mystruct'\n```\nFix: Define the structure before using it.\n\n**\"unknown const\"**\n```\ndev_mydev.txt:15: unknown const 'MYIOCTL'\n```\nFix: Add constant to .txt.const file.\n\n**\"duplicate syscall\"**\n```\ndev_mydev.txt:20: duplicate syscall 'ioctl$NAME'\n```\nFix: Use unique names for each ioctl variant.\n\n**\"wrong direction\"**\n```\ndev_mydev.txt:25: ioctl uses ptr[in] but expects ptr[out]\n```\nFix: Match ptr[] direction to ioctl type (_IOR/_IOW/_IOWR).\n\n### Warning Files\n\n`.txt.warn` files contain non-fatal warnings:\n```bash\ncat /opt/syzkaller/sys/linux/dev_mydev.txt.warn\n```\n\nWarnings might indicate:\n- Unused structures\n- Unreferenced constants\n- Suspicious patterns\n\n## Verification Steps\n\nAfter creating new descriptions:\n\n```bash\ncd /opt/syzkaller\n\n# Step 1: Syntax validation\nmake descriptions\n# Should complete without errors\n\n# Step 2: Full build\nmake all TARGETOS=linux TARGETARCH=amd64\n# Should compile all binaries successfully\n\n# Step 3: Check for warnings\ncat sys/linux/dev_mydev.txt.warn\n# Review any warnings\n\n# Step 4: Test with fuzzer (optional)\n# bin/syz-manager -config=myconfig.cfg\n```\n\n## Parallel Builds\n\nSpeed up builds with parallel jobs:\n```bash\nmake -j$(nproc) all TARGETOS=linux TARGETARCH=amd64\n```\n\n## Clean Builds\n\nRemove generated files:\n```bash\nmake clean\n```\n\nFull rebuild:\n```bash\nmake clean\nmake all TARGETOS=linux TARGETARCH=amd64\n```\n\n## Troubleshooting\n\n### Build fails with \"cannot find package\"\n```bash\n# Update Go dependencies\ngo mod tidy\ngo mod download\n```\n\n### Const generation fails\n```bash\n# Check kernel headers are installed\nls /usr/include/linux/ppdev.h\n\n# Install if missing\napt-get install linux-headers-$(uname -r)\n```\n\n### Description validation fails\n```bash\n# Check syntax in .txt file\n# Read error message carefully\n# Compare with similar working descriptions\n```\n\n## Best Practices\n\n1. **Always run `make descriptions` first** - Fast syntax check\n2. **Create .txt.const manually for review** - Understand values\n3. **Run `make all` for final verification** - Complete validation\n4. **Check .txt.warn files** - Review warnings\n5. **Test on target architecture** - Use matching TARGETARCH\n6. **Keep descriptions simple** - Start minimal, expand later\n7. Syscall descriptions\n│       ├── *.txt.const     # Constant values\n│       └── *.txt.warn      # Warnings from generation\n├── bin/                    # Built binaries\n├── pkg/                    # Go packages\n└── Makefile               # Main build file\n```\n\n## Key Make Targets\n\n### 1. make descriptions\nParses and validates syscall descriptions without generating constants.\n\n```bash\ncd /opt/syzkaller\nmake descriptions\n```\n\n**What it does:**\n- Parses all .txt files in sys/linux/\n- Validates syntax\n- Checks for errors in structure definitions\n- Does NOT compile binaries\n\n**Use when:**\n- You've created/modified .txt files\n- You want to check syntax before generating constants\n- Quick validation\n\n### 2. make generate\nGenerates const files and updates descriptions.\n\n```bash\ncd /opt/syzkaller\nmake generate TARGETOS=linux TARGETARCH=amd64\n```\n\n**What it does:**\n- Extracts constants from kernel headers\n- Generates .txt.const files\n- Updates generated code\n\n**Use when:**\n- You have new .txt files needing .txt.const files\n- You've updated kernel headers\n\n### 3. make all\nFull build of syzkaller binaries.\n\n```bash\ncd /opt/syzkaller\nmake all TARGETOS=linux TARGETARCH=amd64\n```\n\n**What it does:**\n- Runs `make descriptions`\n- Compiles all Go code\n- Builds syz-manager, syz-fuzzer, syz-executor\n- Validates everything\n\n**Use when:**\n- Final verification\n- Before running fuzzer\n- After any changes\n\n### 4. make extract\nExtracts constants from kernel headers.\n\n```bash\ncd /opt/syzkaller\nmake extract TARGETOS=linux TARGETARCH=amd64\n```\n\n**Use when:**\n- Need to regenerate .txt.const files from scratch\n\n## Build Variables\n\n### TARGETOS\nOperating system target:\n- `linux` - Linux kernel\n- `freebsd` - FreeBSD\n- `netbsd` - NetBSD\n- etc.\n\n### TARGETARCH\nArchitecture target:\n- `amd64` - 64-bit x86\n- `386` - 32-bit x86\n- `arm64` - 64-bit ARM\n- `arm` - 32-bit ARM\n\n### Example\n```bash\nmake all TARGETOS=linux TARGETARCH=386\nmake all TARGETOS=linux TARGETARCH=amd64\n```\n\n## Common Workflows\n\n### Workflow 1: Adding New Device Description\n\n```bash\n# 1. Create .txt file\nvim /opt/syzkaller/sys/linux/dev_mydev.txt\n\n# 2. Validate syntax\ncd /opt/syzkaller\nmake descriptions\n\n# 3. Create .txt.const manually or generate\n# (Manual for this exercise)\n\n# 4. Full build to verify\nmake all TARGETOS=linux TARGETARCH=amd64\n```\n\n### Workflow 2: Updating Existing Description\n\n```bash\n# 1. Edit .txt file\nvim /opt/syzkaller/sys/linux/dev_mydev.txt\n\n# 2. Quick syntax check\nmake descriptions\n\n# 3. Full verification\nmake all TARGETOS=linux TARGETARCH=amd64\n```\n\n### Workflow 3: Multi-architecture Build\n\n```bash\n# Build for multiple architectures\nmake all TARGETOS=linux TARGETARCH=amd64\nmake all TARGETOS=linux TARGETARCH=386\nmake all TARGETOS=linux TARGETARCH=arm64\n```\n\n## Error Messages\n\n### Common Errors\n\n**\"unknown type\"**\n```\ndev_mydev.txt:10: unknown type 'mystruct'\n```\nFix: Define the structure before using it.\n\n**\"unknown const\"**\n```\ndev_mydev.txt:15: unknown const 'MYIOCTL'\n```\nFix: Add constant to .txt.const file.\n\n**\"duplicate syscall\"**\n```\ndev_mydev.txt:20: duplicate syscall 'ioctl$NAME'\n```\nFix: Use unique names for each ioctl variant.\n\n**\"wrong direction\"**\n```\ndev_mydev.txt:25: ioctl uses ptr[in] but expects ptr[out]\n```\nFix: Match ptr[] direction to ioctl type (_IOR/_IOW/_IOWR).\n\n### Warning Files\n\n`.txt.warn` files contain non-fatal warnings:\n```bash\ncat /opt/syzkaller/sys/linux/dev_mydev.txt.warn\n```\n\nWarnings might indicate:\n- Unused structures\n- Unreferenced constants\n- Suspicious patterns\n\n## Verification Steps\n\nAfter creating new descriptions:\n\n```bash\ncd /opt/syzkaller\n\n# Step 1: Syntax validation\nmake descriptions\n# Should complete without errors\n\n# Step 2: Full build\nmake all TARGETOS=linux TARGETARCH=amd64\n# Should compile all binaries successfully\n\n# Step 3: Check for warnings\ncat sys/linux/dev_mydev.txt.warn\n# Review any warnings\n\n# Step 4: Test with fuzzer (optional)\n# bin/syz-manager -config=myconfig.cfg\n```\n\n## Parallel Builds\n\nSpeed up builds with parallel jobs:\n```bash\nmake -j$(nproc) all TARGETOS=linux TARGETARCH=amd64\n```\n\n## Clean Builds\n\nRemove generated files:\n```bash\nmake clean\n```\n\nFull rebuild:\n```bash\nmake clean\nmake all TARGETOS=linux TARGETARCH=amd64\n```\n\n## Troubleshooting\n\n### Build fails with \"cannot find package\"\n```bash\n# Update Go dependencies\ngo mod tidy\ngo mod download\n```\n\n### Const generation fails\n```bash\n# Check kernel headers are installed\nls /usr/include/linux/ppdev.h\n\n# Install if missing\napt-get install linux-headers-$(uname -r)\n```\n\n### Description validation fails\n```bash\n# Check syntax in .txt file\n# Read error message carefully\n# Compare with similar working descriptions\n```\n\n## Best Practices\n\n1. **Always run `make descriptions` first** - Fast syntax check\n2. **Create .txt.const manually for review** - Understand values\n3. **Run `make all` for final verification** - Complete validation\n4. **Check .txt.warn files** - Review warnings\n5. **Test on target architecture** - Use matching TARGETARCH\n6. **Keep descriptions simple** - Start minimal, expand later\n7. **Version control** - Commit working versions\n","structuredPatch":[],"originalFile":null}}
